<a id="item-1"></a>
## [PlanFlip: New Attacks Exploit Planning Phase in Multi-Agent LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.16199) ⭐️ 9.0/10

Researchers introduced PlanFlip, a framework of four planning-phase prompt injection attacks—GoalSubstitution, PriorityInversion, ContextPollution, and RoleConfusion—that target multi-agent LLM systems, achieving cascade amplification by corrupting the Planner's context. Testing on nine frontier LLMs across 3,479 episodes revealed that stronger models like GPT-5 are more vulnerable, with an attack success rate of 0.68. This work identifies a critical security blind spot in multi-agent LLM systems, showing that planning-phase attacks can simultaneously corrupt all downstream sub-tasks, and that stronger models are not inherently more secure. It challenges the assumption that capability and security align, urging the community to reconsider defense strategies for agentic AI. The four attacks are disguised as plausible tool outputs to evade keyword filters. Notably, homogeneous pipelines (e.g., GPT-4o and Llama-3.3-70B) exhibit a correlated-agent blind spot where attacks restructure plans while the same-backbone Critic reports alignment, with semantic deviation as low as -0.20 to -0.32. Reasoning-augmented models like DeepSeek-R1 resisted all attacks, achieving StepShift = 0.00.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 21, 04:00

**Background**: Multi-agent LLM systems use a Planner to decompose user goals into sub-tasks, which Executor and Critic agents then carry out and audit. Prompt injection attacks exploit the model's inability to distinguish between legitimate and malicious inputs, and in multi-agent setups, a single injection can cascade through the pipeline. Previous work has shown prompt injection vulnerabilities in single-agent systems, but the planning phase of multi-agent architectures had not been systematically studied.

## [OpenMHC: Largest Open Wearable Health Dataset Released](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.16235) ⭐️ 9.0/10

OpenMHC releases the largest open-access wearable health dataset with over 60 million hours of data from 11,894 participants, along with open-source implementations of wearable foundation models and a unified benchmark. This democratizes wearable health AI research by providing unprecedented scale of open data and reproducible models, enabling the community to develop and compare health monitoring algorithms more effectively. The dataset includes 19 sensor channels (e.g., step count, heart rate, sleep, workouts) and up to 169 linked variables covering health, lifestyle, mood, and behavior. The benchmark covers three tracks: downstream prediction, multivariate imputation, and time-series forecasting.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 21, 04:00

**Background**: Wearable devices like smartwatches enable continuous health monitoring, but large datasets are often proprietary, hindering research. Foundation models trained on such data have shown promise but are rarely open-weight. OpenMHC addresses this by releasing data from the My Heart Counts study, which used Apple's ResearchKit.

## [AI Generates Novel Proofs in Banach Space Theory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.17388) ⭐️ 9.0/10

Researchers used large language models to generate key ideas and proofs for five new results in Banach space theory, which were then verified and refined by human mathematicians. This demonstrates that AI can contribute to genuine mathematical discovery, not just solve textbook problems, potentially accelerating research in pure mathematics and other fields. The AI system also includes an automated component that searches the literature for open problems and attempts solutions at scale, highlighting a pipeline for AI-assisted research.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 21, 04:00

**Background**: Banach space theory is a branch of functional analysis that studies complete normed vector spaces. Automated theorem proving has long aimed to prove mathematical theorems using computer programs, but generating genuinely novel proofs has remained challenging. This work shows that modern language models can produce original mathematical reasoning when guided by human verification.

<a id="item-4"></a>
## [Lookahead Sparse Attention Cuts GPU Memory 86.5% for Long Context LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.09079) ⭐️ 9.0/10

Researchers propose Lookahead Sparse Attention (LSA), a new inference paradigm that uses a Neural Memory Indexer to proactively predict and cache only query-critical KV chunks, reducing GPU memory footprint to 13.5% of baseline while maintaining or improving accuracy. This breakthrough directly addresses the GPU memory bottleneck for ultra-long context LLM serving, enabling 2.8× throughput and 2.7× concurrency gains on 8×H20 GPUs, which could significantly reduce deployment costs and enable new applications requiring very long contexts. The Neural Memory Indexer is trained independently via a backbone-free decoupled training strategy, avoiding loading the massive backbone model into GPU memory. At 1M context, per-decode-token compute drops to 0.30× of baseline and GPU KV cache shrinks by 90% (from 3.73 GB to 0.37 GB).

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 21, 04:00

**Background**: Large language models (LLMs) use a KV cache to store key-value pairs from previous tokens during decoding, which grows linearly with context length. For ultra-long contexts (e.g., 1M tokens), this cache consumes massive GPU memory, limiting throughput and concurrency. Traditional sparse attention methods still require loading the full cache or rely on heuristics that may miss important tokens.

<details><summary>References</summary>
<ul>
<li><a href="https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.09079">[2606.09079] FlashMemory-DeepSeek-V4: Lightning Index Ultra-Long Context via Lookahead Sparse Attention</a></li>
<li><a href="https://arxiv.org/html/2606.09079v1">FlashMemory-DeepSeek-V4: Lightning Index Ultra-Long Context via Lookahead Sparse Attention</a></li>

## [Apple Wins CSAM Scanning Lawsuit, Judge Criticizes Law](https://blog.ericgoldman.org/archives/2026/07/apple-defeats-liability-for-not-scanning-icloud-for-csam-but-the-judge-was-not-pleased-amy-v-apple.htm) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A U.S. court ruled that Apple is not liable for failing to scan iCloud for Child Sexual Abuse Material (CSAM), dismissing a lawsuit brought by a victim of child sexual abuse. The judge expressed dissatisfaction with the legal framework that allows tech companies to prioritize encryption over scanning. This ruling reinforces the legal protection for end-to-end encryption, a key privacy feature, but highlights the ongoing tension between privacy rights and child safety. It could influence future legislation and public debate on whether tech companies should be required to scan encrypted data for illegal content. The judge described the outcome as 'disturbing,' noting that victimized children become 'collateral damage' of privacy protections. Apple's Advanced Data Protection offers end-to-end encryption for iCloud, which prevents Apple from scanning photos, a capability that other cloud services like Google Photos use to detect CSAM.

hackernews · speckx · Jul 21, 14:31 · [Discussion](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=48992870)

**Background**: Child Sexual Abuse Material (CSAM) refers to images or videos depicting the sexual abuse of children. Tech companies typically detect CSAM by scanning uploaded files against a database of known illegal images. Apple introduced end-to-end encryption for iCloud in 2022, which prevents the company from accessing user data, including the ability to scan for CSAM. This case tested whether companies can be held liable for not scanning encrypted data.

<a id="item-6"></a>
## [Jane Street Open-Sources Incremental Computation Library](https://github.com/janestreet/incremental) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Jane Street has open-sourced Incremental, an OCaml library for incremental computation that efficiently recomputes only the affected parts of a computation graph when inputs change. This library brings principled incremental computation to OCaml, enabling performance-critical applications like financial trading systems to react to data changes with minimal recomputation, and its open-source release allows the broader community to adopt and build upon it. Incremental is built on a dependency graph where nodes are marked dirty on input changes, and recomputation is lazy, occurring only when a dependent node's value is requested. The library is used internally at Jane Street and powers their Bonsai UI framework.

hackernews · handfuloflight · Jul 21, 03:50 · [Discussion](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=48987822)

**Background**: Incremental computation is a technique that, when data changes, only recomputes outputs that depend on the changed data, saving time compared to full recomputation. This is similar to how build systems like Make work, but generalized to arbitrary computations. The library is written in OCaml, a functional programming language known for its strong type system and performance.

<details><summary>References</summary>
<ul>
<li><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Incremental_computing">Incremental computing - Wikipedia</a></li>
<li><a href="https://github.com/janestreet/incremental?ref=upstract.com">GitHub - janestreet / incremental at upstract.com · GitHub</a></li>
<li><a href="https://news.oblaidish.com/tx/janestreet-incremental-library">Jane Street releases Incremental , an OCaml library for incremental ...</a></li>

</ul>
</details>

**Discussion**: Commenters noted parallels with JavaScript signals (used in Vue, SolidJS, etc.) and with Goldman Sachs' historical 'Node Purpling' approach. Others linked Incremental to Differential Dataflow and DBSP, and pointed to Jane Street's Bonsai UI library built on top of Incremental.

<a id="item-9"></a>
## [Rater State Bias in RLHF Preference Data: An Audit Framework](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.16195) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper identifies rater state (e.g., stress, fatigue) as a structured confound in RLHF preference data and proposes an audit framework with falsifiable predictions to detect such bias. If rater state bias is real, it could systematically skew reward models and aligned policies, undermining the reliability of RLHF-based alignment. This work provides a concrete method to audit for such bias, which is crucial for AI safety and trustworthy human feedback systems. The paper defines rater state shift, rater state confound, and correlated rater state bias, and introduces survival level emotional authenticity as a measurable response pattern. It derives five falsifiable predictions and effect size thresholds for an initial audit, along with a pilot study plan using publicly available instruction-tuned models.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 21, 04:00

**Background**: RLHF (Reinforcement Learning from Human Feedback) is a common method to align language models with human preferences by training a reward model on pairwise preference labels from human raters. However, these labels may be influenced by factors beyond output quality, such as the rater's emotional or physical state, introducing structured bias that standard noise models do not capture.

## [LLMs Show Consistent Risk Attitudes Across Domains](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.16197) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new study introduces a cross-domain framework to measure risk attitudes in LLMs, finding that six models exhibit stable belief-to-decision mappings and cross-domain rank-order stability across spatial, clinical, and financial tasks. This reveals risk attitude as a stable and previously uncharacterized dimension of LLM behavior, crucial for AI safety and deployment in high-stakes settings like healthcare and finance. The study tested six LLMs and 100 humans using regression models to extract belief-to-decision mappings, quantifying risk sensitivity and risk attitude bias; LLMs showed convergence toward a restricted risk-attitude distribution relative to humans.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 21, 04:00

**Background**: Risk attitude refers to how an agent translates perceived risk into decisions under uncertainty. Prior work has examined biases in LLMs, but systematic measurement of risk attitudes across domains was lacking. This study decouples contextual risk belief from categorical decision, enabling cross-domain comparison.

## [Survey on GNN-based Link Prediction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.16198) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper presents a comprehensive survey of GNN-based link prediction, introducing a novel taxonomy that categorizes methods by encoder architectures (GCN, GAE, GAT, GFormer) and applications in knowledge graphs and recommendation systems. This survey fills a critical gap by systematically reviewing GNN architectures for link prediction, providing researchers with a structured understanding of current techniques and future directions, which is highly relevant to the AI/ML community. The taxonomy covers four key encoder architectures: GCN-based, GAE-based, GAT-based, and GFormer-based methods, each with discussed strengths and limitations. The survey also highlights applications in knowledge graphs and recommendation systems, along with current challenges and promising future directions.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 21, 04:00

**Background**: Graph Neural Networks (GNNs) are deep learning models designed to process graph-structured data, such as social networks or molecular structures. Link prediction is a fundamental task in graph analysis that aims to infer missing edges or predict future connections. GNN-based methods have become the leading approach for link prediction, but existing reviews lacked a systematic focus on underlying GNN architectures. This survey addresses that gap by proposing a dedicated taxonomy from a GNN perspective.

<a id="item-12"></a>
## [Agrepl: Deterministic Replay for AI Agent Systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.16200) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers introduced agrepl, a CLI framework that enables deterministic replay of AI agent executions by intercepting all external interactions via a MITM proxy and replaying them in isolation. This addresses the critical non-determinism problem in LLM-based agents, enabling reliable debugging and reproducibility, which is essential for production-grade agent systems. Agrepl achieves 100% replay fidelity (F=1.0) and 98.3% median per-step latency reduction across five workloads, and is implemented in Go as a single static binary under MIT license.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 21, 04:00

**Background**: AI agent systems that combine LLMs with external tools are inherently non-deterministic due to LLM sampling variance, API state changes, and environment noise. Existing observability platforms can log executions but cannot reproduce them faithfully. Deterministic replay solves this by recording all non-deterministic sources and substituting them during replay, turning unreproducible runs into debuggable sessions.

## [W2SPO: Weak-to-Strong Off-Policy RL with 8 Tokens](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.16205) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers propose W2SPO, an off-policy reinforcement learning method that injects short auxiliary segments (as few as 8 tokens) from a weak auxiliary model into target model trajectories to overcome exploration bottlenecks in reasoning tasks. This addresses a critical support-limited exploration bottleneck in RL for LLMs, where target models often converge to erroneous reasoning basins. W2SPO achieves superior performance on math reasoning benchmarks with a 3.55× training speedup over vanilla GRPO. W2SPO restricts policy updates to the short inserted segments based on final verifiable rewards, while the target model completes the reasoning path from diverted states. At 4B scale, it improves Pass@1 from 62.3% to 64.2% under the same sampling budget.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 21, 04:00

**Background**: Reinforcement learning with verifiable rewards is a standard approach for enhancing reasoning in LLMs, but it suffers from a support-limited bottleneck: target model samples often converge to the same erroneous reasoning basins, providing negligible reward contrast. Off-policy RL allows using data generated by arbitrary policies rather than the current evaluating policy, enabling more efficient exploration. Weak-to-strong learning leverages a weaker model to guide a stronger model's training, which is particularly useful when the target model surpasses human capabilities.

## [Symbolic Augmentation Fixes Fact-Checker Blind Spot](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.16212) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers identified that neural fact-checkers fail to recognize physically equivalent quantities like 95°C and 368.15 K, achieving only 36.5% accuracy on such canonical-equivalence rewrites. They propose Symbolic Augmentation, a training-time framework that generates label-preserving augmented data by reversing a symbolic verifier, lifting robustness to 98.2%. This blind spot could silently invert scientific claims in summaries, undermining trust in AI-assisted fact-checking. The proposed method bridges symbolic and neural approaches, achieving closed-frontier LLM-level performance at no inference cost, with transfer improvements on external benchmarks. The paper introduces a five-class typed-quantity error taxonomy and a 1500-item benchmark (Krippendorff's alpha = 0.882). The augmented ModernBERT encoder achieves macro-F1 of 0.902 on in-distribution data and 0.828 on SciFact-Open, while symbolic features as auxiliary inputs and symbolic silver labels under teacher noise both yield negative results.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 21, 04:00

**Background**: Neural fact-checkers often rely on large language models that can hallucinate numbers and units. Canonical equivalence refers to different textual representations of the same physical quantity (e.g., 95°C vs 368.15 K). Symbolic augmentation uses a symbolic verifier's reverse process to create diverse training examples that preserve the ground truth label.

RAIL Guard introduces a closed-loop evaluation-to-remediation pipeline for LLM agents, achieving 96.9% convergence compared to 49.1% for block-and-retry, with feedback-driven self-repair showing 86.6% convergence on fixable dimensions without significant utility loss. This work addresses a critical gap in responsible AI by moving beyond binary blocking to iterative remediation, significantly improving safety convergence while minimizing utility loss. It provides a practical, open-source framework for deploying safer LLM agents in production. The pipeline evaluates outputs across eight dimensions and uses an evaluate-rewrite-reevaluate loop. Pre-tool-call evaluation reduces unsafe agent executions by 33% with zero impact on task completion, and the system is available as open-source SDKs.

## [LaCache: Lossless Caching & Mixed Precision for Diffusion LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.16339) ⭐️ 8.0/10

LaCache is a training-free acceleration framework for Diffusion Large Language Models (DLLMs) that caches intermediate results (EmbedCache, RoPECache, FACache) and applies per-group FP8 quantization to FFN layers, achieving up to 1.3× speedup alone and up to 40.2× when combined with other methods. This work addresses operator-level redundancy in DLLMs, a key bottleneck for efficient parallel text generation, and provides a practical, lossless acceleration technique that can be combined with existing methods to significantly reduce inference latency. LaCache uses Lossless State Memoization (LSM) to cache three types of intermediate results: EmbedCache for embedding outputs, RoPECache for pre-attention states, and FACache for online softmax statistics in FlashAttention. The per-group FP8 quantization adapts to step-dependent activation distributions across the diffusion process.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 21, 04:00

**Background**: Diffusion Large Language Models (DLLMs) generate text through an iterative denoising process using bidirectional context and parallel decoding, enabling faster generation than autoregressive models. However, they suffer from operator-level redundancy because they recompute the entire sequence at each denoising step, even though parts of the sequence remain unchanged. Semi-Autoregressive (SAR) decoding allows parallel generation of multiple tokens, but still recomputes unchanged prefixes and suffixes. FlashAttention uses online softmax to compute attention efficiently in a tiled manner, but its statistics are not reused across steps.

A new paper formalizes Exact Network Surgery, a method to insert residual blocks into computational graphs while preserving the function bit-exactly and ensuring immediate trainability of inserted parameters. This work overcomes limitations of prior methods like Net2Net, which tolerated numerical perturbations or required full rebuilds, enabling more efficient and reliable model growth for large-scale deep learning. ## [CART Framework Mitigates Error Snowballing in MLLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.16727) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers propose Constraint-Anchored Reasoning Traces (CART), a neuro-symbolic framework that interleaves natural language reasoning with machine-checkable symbolic constraints to prevent error propagation in autoregressive multimodal LLMs. On five benchmarks, CART reduces the snowball rate from 0.65 to 0.14 and improves GQA accuracy by +4.6 percentage points. Error snowballing is a critical weakness in chain-of-thought reasoning for multimodal LLMs, and CART provides a principled, lightweight solution that combines the flexibility of natural language with the rigor of symbolic verification. This approach could significantly improve the reliability of multimodal reasoning systems in high-stakes applications. CART includes a constraint propagation module combining a learned neural grounding head with Boolean Constraint Propagation, and a backtrack controller that reverts to the last consistent checkpoint upon contradiction detection. The framework uses a variable-frequency emission mechanism to adaptively control anchor density, and was trained on 218K instances from GQA, CLEVR-CoGenT, and VCR using LoRA fine-tuning.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 21, 04:00

**Background**: Autoregressive multimodal LLMs generate tokens sequentially, and errors in early reasoning steps can cascade, corrupting all subsequent outputs—a phenomenon known as error snowballing. Chain-of-thought reasoning exacerbates this issue because each step depends on previous ones. Neuro-symbolic approaches combine neural networks with symbolic reasoning to leverage the strengths of both, but prior work often sacrifices natural language flexibility or catches errors too late.

## [Logical Graphs Improve LLM Uncertainty Quantification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.16868) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers propose Logical Graph Uncertainty (LGU), a framework that models logical relationships like implication and incompatibility among LLM answers, outperforming semantic entropy by up to +7.1% AUROC and +3.5% AUARC across benchmarks. LGU addresses a key limitation of existing uncertainty metrics that overestimate uncertainty when answers are diverse but logically compatible, which is crucial for safety-critical applications like medical diagnosis or legal advice. LGU aggregates probability mass along entailment chains, computes entropy over logically maximal hypotheses, and penalizes mutual incompatibility. It uses two quantities: Implication Graph Entropy (IGE) and Logical Graph Uncertainty (LGU).

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 21, 04:00

**Background**: Large Language Models (LLMs) often produce confidently stated but unreliable outputs. Existing uncertainty metrics like semantic entropy measure agreement at the level of semantic equivalence but ignore logical relationships between distinct answers, leading to overestimation of uncertainty when responses are diverse yet logically compatible (e.g., differing only in granularity).

<a id="item-33"></a>
## [Active Inference Equivalence to ρ-POMDPs Eliminates Tuning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.16981) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper proves that minimizing Expected Free Energy (EFE) in active inference is exactly equivalent to solving a ρ-POMDP with a fixed exploration weight of w=1, eliminating the need for manual tuning of exploration bonuses. This theoretical equivalence provides a principled, out-of-the-box exploration objective for decision-making under partial observability, impacting AI, robotics, and applications like fault detection and medical screening where balancing information cost and reward is critical. The equivalence holds for observe-then-commit POMDPs and extends to factored observation POMDPs, covering interleaved observe-act problems like non-destructive testing. Experiments on Tiger, RockSample, and a Structural Inspection benchmark with over 65,000 states show the untuned weight matches or outperforms reward-only planning and avoids over-exploration.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 21, 04:00

**Background**: Partially Observable Markov Decision Processes (POMDPs) model decision-making when the agent cannot directly observe the full state. Standard POMDPs value information only through its effect on reward, while ρ-POMDPs allow direct reward for uncertainty reduction via a belief-dependent utility ρ, but require hand-tuning of the exploration weight. Active inference is a Bayesian framework that unifies perception and action by minimizing variational free energy, naturally balancing exploration and exploitation through Expected Free Energy (EFE).

Researchers collected 6,246 HDL Q&A posts from Stack Overflow, built a taxonomy, and conducted a user study with 19 engineers, finding that LLMs over-answer with redundant alternatives (65.7%) and verbose padding (69.1%), and proposed a multi-agent framework to improve answer quality. This work highlights critical quality issues in LLM-generated HDL answers, which can lead to timing violations or non-synthesizable logic in hardware design, and provides a dataset and framework to mitigate these risks. ## [OTAP: Structure-Aware Optimal Transport for Agent Trajectory Evaluation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.17082) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers propose OTAP, a novel metric that evaluates LLM agent trajectories by computing an unbalanced fused Gromov-Wasserstein distance between the agent's execution graph and a set of valid solution graphs, enabling invariance to dependency-preserving reorderings and robustness to redundant steps. OTAP addresses critical limitations of current agent evaluation methods—binary success flags and exact matching—by providing a granular, structure-aware distance that can distinguish valid from invalid trajectories even when semantics-only metrics fail, potentially improving agent training and benchmarking. OTAP is a pseudo-metric with provable invariance to dependency-preserving reorderings and bounded sensitivity to redundant steps; its unbalanced marginals handle missing or hallucinated steps without forcing a match, and its soft coupling accommodates variations in plan granularity.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 21, 04:00

**Background**: Large language model (LLM) agents generate trajectories that interleave planning, tool calls, and intermediate results. Current evaluation methods either use a binary success flag, which cannot distinguish luck from sound reasoning, or exact matching, which penalizes valid but differently ordered plans. Optimal transport (OT) provides a way to measure distances between distributions, and its variants like fused Gromov-Wasserstein can compare attributed graphs. OTAP leverages these techniques to evaluate agent trajectories as attributed dependency graphs.

<a id="item-43"></a>
## [Panache: One-Pass Streaming for Pan Matrix Profile Motif Discovery](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.17481) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Panache is the first one-pass streaming algorithm for z-normalized pan matrix profile motif discovery, replacing repeated self-joins with a single near-linear scan. It leverages sliding-DFT recurrences and running statistics to maintain the non-DC spectrum of every z-normalized subsequence online. This breakthrough dramatically reduces the computational cost of motif discovery across multiple window lengths, enabling analysis of very long time series in minutes instead of hours. It has the potential to accelerate exploratory data analysis in domains like IoT, finance, and healthcare. Panache uses an occupancy-controlled hash directory where similar subsequences collide, and applies Parseval's theorem to derive a lower bound that rejects most colliding pairs before exact computation. On a 5-million-sample Wafer dataset across 51 lengths, it completes one pass in 2.9 minutes and emits exact motifs in 6.0 minutes, compared to 7.95 hours for the fastest exact CPU baseline.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 21, 04:00

**Background**: Motif discovery finds recurring patterns in time series, but the pattern duration is often unknown, so analysts must try many window lengths. The pan matrix profile (PMP) computes a matrix profile for each length, traditionally requiring L quadratic self-joins. Z-normalization ensures shape-based similarity by subtracting the mean and dividing by the standard deviation of each subsequence.

Researchers propose ARBITER, a novel LLM guardrail framework that uses dual-hypothesis reasoning—explicitly considering both safe and unsafe interpretations of a prompt—and multi-component supervised fine-tuning (MC-SFT) to outperform expensive methods while being cost-effective. ARBITER addresses the critical need for transparent and affordable AI safety mechanisms, enabling wider adoption of guardrails in production LLM systems without relying on large or closed-source models. ARBITER uses a self-generation strategy for reasoning traces and LoRA-based parameter-efficient fine-tuning, achieving better performance than expensive approaches on three safety moderation benchmarks, with clear gains in out-of-domain evaluations.

## [First Controlled Study of Progressive Disclosure for Long-Context Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.17598) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new paper presents the first controlled study comparing progressive disclosure with raw-document navigation and hybrid retrieval for long-document question answering in agentic AI systems, using Agent Skills packs on InfiniteBench. This study provides empirical evidence for a widely adopted but previously anecdotal pattern, showing that progressive disclosure can significantly outperform raw-document navigation when scaling to multi-book tasks, while being redundant for strong single-book agents. The study tested three agent harnesses and three model families on InfiniteBench, finding that one level of progressive disclosure is sufficient and a second routing level never helps. On single-book tasks, gains depend on the harness; on multi-book tasks, raw-document navigation collapses while progressive disclosure degrades more slowly.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 21, 04:00

**Background**: Long-document question answering typically requires either loading the entire document into the context window or using a separate retriever. Agentic AI introduces a third option: giving the agent the document path and letting it decide how to read. Progressive disclosure, enabled by Agent Skills, exposes only query-relevant content hierarchically, from descriptions to specific passages.

## [FlowBlock: Training-Free Parallel Decoding for Diffusion LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.17652) ⭐️ 8.0/10

FlowBlock introduces a training-free parallel decoding framework for block-wise diffusion LLMs, using gated wavefront scheduling and token-to-token editing to relax inter-block dependencies and achieve up to 4.01× speedup in tokens per second. This work significantly improves inference speed for diffusion LLMs without requiring additional training, addressing a key bottleneck in block-wise decoding and enabling more efficient deployment of self-correcting diffusion models. FlowBlock combines Gated Wavefront Decoding and Heterogeneous Wavefront Packing, achieving up to 2.95× and 4.01× TPS improvement over LLaDA-2.1 and LLaDA-2.0, respectively, while also improving average accuracy by 1.3 points and reducing latency by up to 77.1%.

FlowBlock leverages the self-correcting nature of these models—token-to-token editing can repair drafts from slightly stale context—to turn block finality from a hard dependency into a scheduling resource.

## [Semantic-Answerability Gap in Table RAG Diagnosed](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.17742) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces TCR-Bench, a diagnostic benchmark for table content-level answerability in retrieval-augmented generation (RAG), and reveals a persistent Semantic-Answerability Gap where dense retrievers retrieve semantically similar but logically distinct tables, causing QA performance to drop from 0.755 (oracle) to 0.330 (top-5 retrieved). This gap highlights a fundamental mismatch in table RAG between semantic relevance and answerability, which can severely degrade the reliability of QA systems relying on table sources. The proposed Answerability-Aware Reranking (AAR) pipeline shows that a lightweight answerability verification step can largely recover performance, offering a practical path to improve real-world RAG systems. TCR-Bench is built around sibling tables with highly similar schemas but subtle content differences. The analysis attributes the gap to semantic accumulation, schema-level cue dependence, and weak row-column binding; AAR raises top-1 target retrieval from 18.2% to 57.4%.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 21, 04:00

**Background**: Retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) systems retrieve external knowledge sources, such as tables, to answer queries. However, a retrieved table may not contain sufficient evidence to answer the query—a property called answerability. Traditional retrieval models optimize for semantic relevance, which does not guarantee answerability, leading to a mismatch that this paper systematically diagnoses.

Researchers propose MIND, a cognitive jailbreak framework that reframes adversarial prompt generation for Text-to-Image models as a belief-state inference problem over latent defense mechanisms, achieving a 95.62% attack success rate on Stable Diffusion v1.5. This work introduces a new paradigm for jailbreak attacks that goes beyond heuristic prompt engineering, potentially advancing AI safety research by exposing vulnerabilities in commercial T2I systems like Wan-2.5. MIND integrates three components: a Multi-modal Judge for fine-grained feedback decomposition, a Defense Profiler for iterative belief updating, and a Meta-Memory module for retrieving effective strategies, all unified in a reasoning-driven evolutionary optimization process.

## [Shared Discovery Paradox: Better Rankings, Worse Search](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.18045) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new paper introduces the Shared Discovery Paradox, showing that pooling information into a single ranking can reduce group discovery performance from 0.8322 to 0.3835 compared to decentralized search, despite improving the best single recommendation from 0.20 to 0.3835. This paradox challenges the common practice of centralizing information in organizations and AI systems, revealing that better information can lead to worse outcomes due to protocol design rather than information quality. The model uses 16 boxes, one target, eight searchers, and noisy private clues. A coordinated eight-action portfolio using the same pooled reports achieves 0.8594, while the equal-split game achieves 0.5991, and the price of anarchy is 2 - 1/N.

**Background**: KV cache stores key and value tensors during autoregressive decoding to avoid redundant computation, but its memory footprint grows linearly with sequence length. The RULER benchmark evaluates long-context retrieval capabilities, making it a suitable test for KV-cache quality.

## [Dream-Inspired AI Memory Recombination Boosts Cross-Domain Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.16256) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers implemented a dream-like cross-domain memory recombination mechanism in two unrelated AI systems—a LoRA fine-tuning pipeline (DREAMS) and a symbolic engine (SAPIENCE)—and found that cross-domain consolidation consistently outperforms within-domain rehearsal. The symbolic system discovered novel cross-domain connections at 85.7%, a 21 percentage point gain over baseline, while the neural system improved cross-domain math reasoning by up to 14.5 percentage points on GSM8K. This work challenges the traditional view that memory consolidation merely prevents forgetting, suggesting instead that its primary value lies in recombining knowledge across domains to drive discovery. If validated, it could lead to AI systems that learn more creatively and generalize better, with implications for robotics, scientific discovery, and lifelong learning. The effect was shown to be a genuine property of the model weights, not a prompt artifact—prepending the same material in-context to a 671B-parameter model actually reversed the gain. The authors also validated their prediction against 50,000 real papers and proposed a falsifiable hippocampal-recording prediction to distinguish recombination from rehearsal.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 21, 04:00

**Background**: Memory consolidation in AI typically refers to transferring information from short-term to long-term storage, often through rehearsal or replay. This paper draws inspiration from neuroscience, where dreams are thought to recombine disparate experiences to foster insight. The authors implemented this idea using Low-Rank Adaptation (LoRA), a parameter-efficient fine-tuning method, and a symbolic engine that replays structured knowledge objects.

## [Reducing Per-Sample Harm in Stochastic Optimization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.16261) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper formalizes the concept of 'harm' in stochastic optimization, where batch averaging and optimization history increase individual sample losses, and proposes an efficient proxy that restricts optimization to the last linear layer to mitigate this harm. This work addresses a fundamental issue in deep learning training, potentially improving generalization and reducing per-sample interference, with a method that scales well to large models and inputs. The method reduces the problem's dimensionality to the batch size and leverages the unexpected finding that the last linear layer reliably captures second-order statistics of per-sample gradients, enabling efficient GPU-friendly iterations.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 21, 04:00

**Background**: Stochastic optimization methods like SGD and AdamW combine current mini-batch gradients with historical state (e.g., momentum). While effective, this aggregation can cause parameter updates that increase loss for some individual samples, a phenomenon not previously formalized as 'harm'. The paper introduces a tractable proxy by focusing on the last linear layer, which captures essential gradient statistics.

Researchers introduced Octopus, a multi-scale autonomous discovery engine that combines zero-leakage LLM swarms with physics-based models to mechanistically identify colorectal cancer vulnerabilities. In a fully unsupervised sweep, it autonomously identified IGF2 as a bounded vulnerability to 5-Fluorouracil resistance, validated in vivo. This work bridges the gap between multi-agent reasoning and mathematically bounded clinical survival, establishing a verifiable, zero-leakage paradigm for automated end-to-end biomedical discovery. It could accelerate drug target identification and personalized medicine by combining semantic reasoning with causal constraints. Octopus uses a neuro-symbolic architecture uniting local LLM swarms with algorithmic physics engines, and validated its findings using CRISPR dependency data, XGBoost SHAP vectors, PDX mouse models, and human survival data. The IGF2 discovery maintained significance after Benjamini-Hochberg correction (q=0.0292) and predicted tumor volume shrinkage in mice (p=0.0373).

Researchers propose Neural Depth Field (NDF), a test-time optimization framework that treats a pretrained depth estimator as both a one-shot predictor and a scene-level implicit neural field, enabling consistent and high-fidelity 3D scene geometry inpainting and reconstruction. NDF addresses two critical problems in 3D geometry inpainting—inconsistency with observed geometry and unreliability on out-of-distribution data—by unifying depth estimation and implicit neural fields, achieving state-of-the-art performance with a 63.3% reduction in cross-view inconsistency and a 23.1% improvement in inpainting accuracy. NDF operates via a single test-time optimization that adapts the depth estimator to the target domain while fitting existing geometry to maintain consistency. It demonstrates strong results across diverse scene data, from indoor scans to satellite imagery.

Researchers propose the Group-Contrastive Forward-Forward (GCFF) algorithm, a biologically plausible local learning rule that produces monosemantic neurons with hierarchical abstraction, challenging the dominant sparse autoencoder paradigm in mechanistic interpretability. This work offers a potential paradigm shift in mechanistic interpretability by addressing key limitations of sparse autoencoders, such as non-identifiability and reliance on linear representations, and provides a biologically grounded alternative that may lead to more interpretable AI systems. GCFF combines class-specific routing with within-class contrastive objectives, achieving monosemanticity through architectural constraints rather than sparsity, and can capture non-linear concepts by attaching multiple non-linear layers to the representation under study.

This work addresses a critical failure mode in VLMs—relying on priors over visual evidence—which is essential for AI safety and robustness, especially as VLMs are deployed in agentic systems that require accurate visual grounding. Results show that visual evidence tools help in some settings but are not a universal remedy, as several models still follow priors even when explicit visual evidence is provided.

AEVAL introduces a CI-integrated, deterministic testing framework for agentic skills, replacing anecdotal evaluation with reproducible quality signals. It uses an executor/grader separation to prevent self-correction bias and provides tiered fix suggestions. This framework addresses a critical gap in agentic system testing, enabling reliable and scalable evaluation of AI agent workflows. It can significantly improve the quality assurance of agentic skills in production environments and marketplaces. AEVAL's key innovation is a structural separation between executor and grader, which prevents the agent from grading its own self-corrected outputs. It also introduces a tiered evidence-grounded fix-suggestion scheme (LV1 causal, LV2 quality) posted as inline merge-request comments.

## [AoA: AST-Based Theorem Proving Agent Cuts Token Use](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.16372) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers propose AoA, an LLM-based theorem proving agent that operates on abstract syntax trees (AST) instead of concrete syntax, using Minilang's AST as JSON to reduce token consumption and API costs. Compared to Amazon's Isabelle Agent, AoA cuts API cost by 2.3–4.7x, uses 2.9–6.9x fewer tokens, and solves more problems on verification benchmarks. This work addresses a key bottleneck in LLM-based interactive theorem proving—high token consumption and API cost—by shifting from concrete syntax to AST representation. It also enables adoption of new proof languages like Minilang that are not well-represented in LLM training data, potentially accelerating formal verification and AI-assisted proving. AoA uses a tree-edit model that fuses proof operations and states into one proof tree, so each operation carries its own subgoal's state readable directly off the tree. The model supplies proofs as JSON representations of Minilang's AST, which is native to tool-calling LLMs, avoiding line-number-based queries and repeated error relocation.

**Background**: Interactive theorem proving (ITP) is used for program verification and formalized mathematics but requires significant manual effort. LLM-based proof agents aim to automate this, but they typically operate on serialized concrete syntax, leading to high token usage and API costs. Minilang is a minimalist proof language designed for neural theorem proving, but its novelty makes it unfamiliar to LLMs trained on existing corpora.

## [New Theory Explains Negative Transfer in Multi-Task Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.16554) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces a Capacity-Redundancy (CR) identity that decomposes multi-task learning performance into joint predictive information, label redundancy via total correlation, and a residual coupling term measuring interference. It also provides a clustering-gap decomposition and a gradient-TC bridge that justifies gradient cosine similarity as a redundancy proxy. This work provides a principled theoretical framework for understanding negative transfer in multi-task learning, which is a fundamental challenge in the field. The insights could guide better model design, such as clustered sharing, to improve performance across tasks. The CR identity decomposes the sum of per-task predictive informations into joint predictive information, label redundancy via total correlation (TC), and a residual coupling term Δ. Empirically, clustered LoRA reduces Δ and outperforms random partitions with statistical significance.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 21, 04:00

**Background**: Multi-task learning (MTL) aims to improve performance by training a single model on multiple related tasks simultaneously. However, when tasks are not sufficiently related or the model's capacity is limited, negative transfer can occur, where performance on some tasks degrades. This paper provides a theoretical lens to understand and mitigate this issue.

## [Mitigating NPU Power Bursts Under Low Battery](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.16555) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new study reveals that aggressive operator fusion in mobile NPU compilers creates monolithic superlayers causing peak-current bursts under low voltage, and proposes a graph-rewriting mitigation that inserts barriers to prevent harmful superlayer merging. This work addresses a practical, under-explored problem in mobile NPU inference under low-voltage conditions, which can degrade user experience during battery depletion. The proposed mitigation reduces peak current by 38% with minimal latency overhead, potentially improving performance stability on millions of devices. On a Snapdragon 8 Gen 3 with MobileNetV4 at 768x768 resolution, the method reduces peak current from 3.12 A to 1.94 A with only 3.76% latency overhead, and shifts the inferred DVFS margin by approximately 173 mV. The technique is a black-box, pre-compilation graph rewrite that does not require access to the vendor compiler internals.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 21, 04:00

**Background**: Mobile NPUs execute neural network inference for real-time tasks like camera processing. Under low battery voltage, a single inference can cause a voltage droop on the power delivery network, triggering dynamic voltage and frequency scaling (DVFS) that increases latency. Operator fusion is a common compiler optimization that merges adjacent operations into a single superlayer to improve performance, but overly aggressive fusion can concentrate power draw and cause large current bursts.

A new paper demonstrates that model uncertainty is anti-correlated with safety in model-based reinforcement learning (MBRL), and proposes replacing it with world feedback signals to reduce collision rates from 26% to as low as 1%. This challenges a common assumption in safe RL and offers a principled alternative, potentially improving safety in autonomous systems and aligning with broader AI alignment efforts like RLHF. Across four world-model architectures, dynamics-based uncertainty penalties increased collision rates from 26% to 34%, while world feedback signals (lidar margin, time-to-collision, outcome-trained feedback model) reduced collisions to 1-14% without retraining the world model or planner.

This paper argues that uncertainty is structurally misaligned with task risk, which lives on constraint boundaries, not prediction error.

## [New Metric PCER Audits Fairness in DP ML](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.16620) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers propose the Privacy-Cost Equity Ratio (PCER), a group fairness metric that normalizes a group's positive prediction rate by its per-group overfitting gap, treating privacy cost as a compensatory fairness input in differentially private machine learning. PCER addresses a critical gap by revealing that fairness audits of privacy-preserving systems must account for who bears the cost of protection, not only who benefits from outcomes. It uncovers patterns missed by outcome-based metrics, such as a double disadvantage for protected groups in COMPAS. PCER uses only per-group train and test accuracy (no shadow models), making it a practical post-hoc audit tool. The metric is validated across six benchmark-attribute combinations under DP-SGD at various privacy budgets, and the overfitting gap proxy is confirmed against a direct membership inference attack.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 21, 04:00

**Background**: Differential privacy (DP) limits membership inference risk in machine learning, but prior work shows DP-SGD can widen accuracy disparities across groups. The compensatory fairness framework argues that a group bearing greater privacy exposure is owed proportionally greater benefit. PCER operationalizes this by normalizing benefit (positive prediction rate) by exposure (overfitting gap, which upper-bounds inference vulnerability).

This paper introduces the value Causal Markov Condition (v-CMC), a causal independence principle for utility, and establishes a duality between probability and utility that generalizes Bellman recursion from linear chains to causal DAGs. This paper extends these concepts to general causal DAGs.

Researchers propose trace-based on-policy distillation (TOPD), a teacher-supervised framework that transfers reasoning ability to diffusion language models without reward estimation, achieving performance comparable to RL-trained models with 4x fewer rollout rounds. TOPD samples on-policy diffusion trajectories from the target dLLM, obtains teacher token distributions on partially denoised states, and updates the model using a token-level Reverse KL divergence objective. On MATH500, it enables SDAR-4B-Chat to match TraDo-4B-Instruct's accuracy with an estimated 96.0x model-compute speedup.

**Background**: Diffusion language models (dLLMs) generate text by iteratively denoising masked tokens, offering an alternative to autoregressive generation. Post-training for reasoning in dLLMs is challenging: supervised fine-tuning requires dense but often off-policy masked states, while reinforcement learning relies on sparse rewards or value modeling. Knowledge distillation transfers knowledge from a teacher model to a student model, and on-policy distillation aligns training with the student's own data distribution.

## [CADENCE: Coverage-Adaptive On-Policy Distillation for Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.16955) ⭐️ 8.0/10

CADENCE introduces a unified on-policy knowledge distillation framework with three novel mechanisms—DRIFT, COVA, and FTB—that specifically address cold-start collapse, state-agnostic scheduling, and reward sparsity in reasoning tasks. On GSM8K and MATH-500, it distills a 0.5B student from a 1.5B teacher to 69.8% pass@1, closing 63.2% of the teacher gap. This work addresses critical failure modes in on-policy distillation for reasoning, a key area for deploying compact models without sacrificing performance. Its coverage-adaptive approach and strong results on standard benchmarks suggest a practical path to high-quality reasoning in resource-constrained settings. CADENCE's DRIFT mechanism uses per-token convex mixtures of forward-KL and reverse-KL surrogates, while COVA adaptively schedules the mixture weight based on student coverage, and FTB boosts gradients at high-entropy tokens. All experiments were run on a single Apple Mac Studio (M-series, 64GB unified memory), demonstrating that principled distillation can achieve strong reasoning without datacenter-scale hardware.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 21, 04:00

**Background**: On-policy knowledge distillation trains a student model on its own generated trajectories, using a teacher's outputs as soft targets. However, it suffers from cold-start collapse (student assigns near-zero mass to teacher-preferred tokens), state-agnostic scheduling (fixed divergence interpolation ignoring student progress), and binary reward sparsity (only pass/fail signals). Forward-KL encourages mode-covering behavior while reverse-KL encourages mode-seeking; balancing them is crucial for effective distillation.

Using proof-hard expander-Tseitin and proof-easy ladder-Tseitin formulas with near-matched density, the study found accuracy gaps ranging from -32 to +20 points and a wrong-signed correctness-versus-conflict association (r=+0.15).

This paper controls for clause density and width to isolate hardness effects.

ThAME proposes a 3D heterogeneous multi-chiplet architecture that combines FeFET non-volatile memory and DRAM volatile memory chiplets with a co-designed compute mapping strategy to address memory bandwidth, scatter-gather traffic, and tail-latency bottlenecks in MoE-based LLM inference. This work directly tackles critical performance bottlenecks in MoE-based LLMs, which are increasingly important for scaling large language models. By achieving up to 15.7x speedup and 9.8x energy efficiency improvement over state-of-the-art counterparts, ThAME could significantly reduce inference costs and latency for large-scale AI deployments. ThAME uses FeFET-based non-volatile memory chiplets for expert weights and DRAM-based volatile memory chiplets for activations, with a specialized Network-on-Chip (NoC) backbone to handle non-deterministic token routing traffic. Experimental results show up to 15.7x speedup and 9.8x energy efficiency improvement over state-of-the-art counterparts.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 21, 04:00

**Background**: Mixture of Experts (MoE) is a neural network architecture that uses multiple specialized sub-networks (experts) and a routing mechanism to activate only a subset of experts per input, enabling larger model capacity without proportional compute increase. However, MoE inference faces bottlenecks: high memory bandwidth to fetch non-contiguous expert weights, non-deterministic scatter-gather traffic from token routing, and tail latency from synchronous expert output aggregation. FeFET (Ferroelectric Field-Effect Transistor) is an emerging non-volatile memory technology that offers fast read/write, low power, and high density, making it suitable for storing expert weights in memory-bound workloads.

Talaria's router ranks placements by model residency, KV locality, and instance pressure, and uses soft reservations to account for likely returns. It also introduces session-prefill (SP) to admit budget-eligible continuations before the active model slot closes, and an instance-local substrate that keeps HBM addresses stable and preserves host-restorable KV.

Generated AIBOMs showed complete coverage of required structural fields but weak representation of model-card, metadata, responsible-use, environmental, limitation, and meaningful-description fields.

## [Distilled RL: New Paradigm for LLM Post-training](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.17247) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers propose Distilled Reinforcement Learning (Distilled RL), a method that integrates teacher supervision into the RL objective to provide fine-grained guidance and selective knowledge transfer for LLM post-training. This approach addresses key limitations of existing post-training paradigms—coarse outcome supervision in RL and unconditional imitation in on-policy distillation—potentially improving LLM alignment and reasoning capabilities. Distilled RL comprises three components: reverse importance sampling with clipping, negative sample reset, and sequence-level geometric normalization. Experiments show it outperforms standard RL and on-policy distillation in both within-family and cross-family settings.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 21, 04:00

**Background**: LLM post-training typically uses reinforcement learning (RL) with outcome rewards or on-policy distillation (OPD) that matches teacher logits via KL divergence. RL suffers from credit assignment due to sparse rewards, while OPD either provides little new knowledge (similar teachers) or ineffective guidance (dissimilar teachers). Distilled RL combines both to overcome these issues.

This addresses a key limitation in robotic imitation learning where multimodal policies are often slowed down by synchronous fusion, improving policy responsiveness and task performance in contact-rich manipulation tasks. Experiments show improvements over synchronous fusion and force-aware baselines under heterogeneous modality latencies.

On a single H100 GPU with vLLM batching, SAGA generates 500,000 temporal edges with controlled anomalies in under 90 minutes, scaling to 100,000 nodes while maintaining clustering coefficients above 0.99. ## [KATA: Kernelized Linear Attention Breaks Capacity Wall](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.17419) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers propose KATA (Kernelized Linear Attention Activations), a framework that uses symmetric cones to derive feature maps for linear attention, achieving constant-time inference while improving associative recall capacity. KATA implementations show up to 11x FlashAttention-2 throughput at 131k tokens and near-perfect recall on long-range tasks. This work addresses a fundamental trade-off in linear attention between capacity and interference, which has limited its use in long-context models. KATA could enable more efficient transformers for tasks requiring strong associative memory, such as reasoning over long documents or code. KATA recovers a parameter-free convex output gate and characterizes associative capacity via the Welch interference floor. It is implemented as fused Triton kernels with two operating points: a flash-attention-style forward and an exact O(T) chunked-state form, achieving up to 11x FlashAttention-2 throughput at 131k tokens.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 21, 04:00

**Background**: Linear attention replaces the softmax with a kernel feature map to achieve linear-time complexity in sequence length, but it often suffers from degraded performance on associative recall tasks. Associative recall is the ability to retrieve a stored value given a cue, which is crucial for reasoning and memory in transformers. The Welch interference floor is a concept from signal processing that bounds the minimum interference achievable in a system, here used to analyze the capacity-interference tradeoff.

## [CoCurve: Training-Free Structured LLM Pruning via Co-Pruning Curvature](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.17568) ⭐️ 8.0/10

CoCurve introduces a training-free structured pruning method for LLMs that jointly prunes attention heads and FFN units by modeling cross-module dependencies via a second-order Taylor expansion of KL divergence. Existing training-free pruning methods score units independently, ignoring the coupling between attention and FFN modules through the residual stream, which can lead to removing jointly indispensable units. CoCurve addresses this by capturing co-pruning interactions, enabling more accurate pruning without fine-tuning, which is critical for efficient LLM deployment. CoCurve constructs a Fisher matrix whose diagonal captures individual saliency and off-diagonal entries represent co-pruning curvature, measuring the extra damage of removing two units together. Under a single-ablation additivity approximation, the full M×M interaction matrix is recovered from M forward passes without pairwise sweeps or gradients, and pruning is solved as a single quadratic program under a shared budget.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 21, 04:00

**Background**: Structured pruning removes entire computational units like attention heads and FFN channels to create smaller, dense models that run efficiently on standard hardware. Most training-free methods rank units independently, assuming the total loss from pruning a set equals the sum of individual losses, but this fails for Transformers where sublayers are coupled via the residual stream. CoCurve uses a second-order Taylor expansion of KL divergence to model these dependencies, building on concepts like Fisher matrices and co-pruning curvature.

This paper proposes a latent-variable factorization to decompose a pretrained language model's response distribution into a router and a generator, uncovering implicit reasoning strategies. This work advances interpretability of large language models by making their internal reasoning strategies explicit, which could lead to better understanding and control of model behavior. It also provides a principled solution to posterior collapse, a key challenge in latent variable modeling of language. The method learns a router r(z|x) and a generator g(y|x,z) such that the generator, initialized from the base model, already captures p(y|x) without z, making standard variational inference prone to posterior collapse. The proposed objective measures fractional information gain relative to the base model's loss and prioritizes tokens with high base model surprisal.

This paper addresses that by designing a variational objective that encourages the latent variable to encode strategy-relevant information.

Researchers introduced WC2026-Agents, a contamination-free benchmark evaluating four frontier LLMs (Claude Opus 4.8, ChatGPT GPT-5.5, Gemini 3.1 Pro, Grok) as autonomous forecasting agents on all 104 matches of the 2026 FIFA World Cup, comparing their predictions to bookmaker odds. This benchmark addresses a critical issue in LLM evaluation—test set contamination—by using future events, and provides an economically grounded baseline (betting markets) to assess not just accuracy but decision quality and calibration, revealing that frontier models often agree on predictions but diverge sharply in betting returns and self-awareness. The benchmark includes 416 forecasts and 414 reflections with verbatim reasoning, ground truth, odds, and a reproducible evaluation suite; none of the four agents beat the market's Brier score, and a naive flat stake on the market favorite outperformed all agents, yet betting ROI ranged from -18% to +10% across agents.

## [ETAS: Effect-Typed Language for Safe Agent Systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.17780) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers have introduced ETAS, a novel programming language that treats agentic behaviors such as tool calls, prompts, and human approvals as first-class semantic elements with static typing and effect tracking. The language separates deterministic computation from agentic nondeterminism and externally visible actions, providing formal semantics for safety and verifiability. ETAS addresses critical challenges in AI safety by enabling compile-time verification of authorization, nondeterminism, recovery, and audit evidence for agent systems. This could lead to more reliable and trustworthy AI agents in applications like autonomous systems and decision-making tools. ETAS uses effect rows to track behavioral indices: an escaping effect row and a persistent abstraction of the typed action trace. It includes a terminating compile-time constraint calculus with type specs, callable specs, and trace specs for allow/deny/temporal constraints, and emits residual obligations when dynamic resources prevent complete static proof.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 21, 04:00

**Background**: Effect typing is a type system technique that tracks side effects (e.g., I/O, state changes) alongside value types, enabling safer code. Agent systems involve nondeterministic behaviors like tool calls and human approvals, which are hard to verify with traditional type systems. ETAS extends effect typing to agent-specific actions, providing a formal foundation for reasoning about agent behavior before execution.

## [MRSNorm: Phasor-Based Normalization Halves Parameters](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.17822) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers introduced Mean Root Square Normalization (MRSNorm), a novel normalization technique that pairs channels into 2D phasors and inverts the traditional RMSNorm scaling by computing L2 magnitudes before L1 averaging. MRSNorm halves the number of learnable parameters while preserving conformal invariance and providing built-in gradient clipping, potentially improving stability and efficiency in transformer architectures and other sequence models. The method constrains activations to a phasor manifold, ensuring gradient homogeneity via the Pythagorean identity. Empirical tests on ResNet with CIFAR-100 show MRSNorm prevents numerical explosion under extreme hyperparameters where standard normalizations fail.

**Background**: Root Mean Square Normalization (RMSNorm) is widely used in modern sequence models to accelerate training, but it suffers from outlier-induced instability and gradient starvation. MRSNorm addresses these issues by structurally pairing channels into phasors and inverting the order of operations.

## [CMP: Gradient-Free Architecture Resists Forgetting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.17944) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers introduce CMP (Cognitive Memory Primitive), a gradient-free architecture using sparse relational codes and competitive memory, which achieves 15-19x better backward transfer than a Transformer with online EWC on a 15-domain text protocol. This challenges the prevailing view that catastrophic forgetting is a training-time defect to be patched, suggesting instead it is a structural consequence of backpropagation; a local, sparse learning rule may inherently resist forgetting without replay or regularization. CMP learns entirely through local, gradient-free updates with no backpropagation, and the paper reports a null result on a vision benchmark and an unresolved failure when combining with a separate accuracy-improving mechanism, disclosed as an honest negative result.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 21, 04:00

**Background**: Catastrophic forgetting is the tendency of neural networks to abruptly forget previously learned information when learning new data. Standard approaches to mitigate it, such as Elastic Weight Consolidation (EWC), add regularization penalties during backpropagation-based training. CMP proposes an alternative: a structurally different architecture that avoids backpropagation entirely.

This paper provides a geometric analysis showing that chain-of-thought (CoT) reasoning smooths the loss landscape in its sharpest direction, stabilizing value conflict resolution in LLMs trained with RLHF. This work offers a novel geometric perspective on how CoT reasoning aids value alignment, a critical challenge in AI safety. The paper demonstrates empirically that value conflict-focused CoT generalizes to different kinds of moral reasoning tasks. ## [Self-State Attacks on Self-Hosted AI Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.17986) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new paper defines a novel class of attacks called self-state attacks, where an AI agent is compromised by corrupting its own memory or configuration files via legitimate OS system calls, and evaluates OS-level defenses against them. This work highlights a fundamental blind spot in OS defenses for self-hosted AI agents, showing that even layered security stacks leave a residual attack surface that is structurally indistinguishable at the OS level, which could impact the security of autonomous AI systems. The attack space is characterized along four axes: Target, Mechanism, Granularity, and Temporal, and instantiated as a 23-cell matrix with 43 concrete operations on real self-state files. The empirical evaluation shows that a layered defense (access control, workload-conditioned detection, and periodic backup) is effective for most cells, but a small residual attack surface remains.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 21, 04:00

**Background**: Self-hosted AI agents run on hardware controlled by the user, reading and writing their own memory and configuration files to function. Unlike cloud-based agents, they rely on local OS mechanisms for security. Self-state attacks exploit the fact that legitimate OS system calls can be used to corrupt an agent's state, making them hard to distinguish from normal operations.

Researchers introduced a 21-scenario benchmark for adaptive multi-round prompt injection attacks on memoryless LLM defenders, showing that allowing 15 rounds of adaptive attacks increases success rates from 0-1% to 5.4-14.0%. ## [Generalised Bellman Recurrence and Three Dualities](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.18077) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new paper derives the Bellman equation from three fundamental conditions—decomposable dynamics, recursive return, and compatible uncertainty aggregation—and reveals three dualities between probability, return, and aggregation, unifying perspectives across reinforcement learning, control, and decision theory. This work provides a unified theoretical foundation for sequential decision-making, potentially enabling cross-domain insights and more principled algorithm design in reinforcement learning, optimal control, and decision theory. The three conditions are: dynamics decomposes through sufficient statistics, return decomposes recursively, and uncertainty aggregation is compatible with both; when one condition fails, tractability can be recovered by augmenting state or deforming return or dynamics.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 21, 04:00

**Background**: The Bellman equation is a cornerstone of dynamic programming and reinforcement learning, expressing the optimal value function recursively. Traditionally, it is derived from the principle of optimality. This paper generalizes the derivation by identifying minimal conditions that give rise to the equation and uncovering dualities that were previously treated separately.

## [Self-Distillation Fixes Rubric RL Failure Mode](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.18082) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new paper identifies 'Suppressed Criteria' as a previously overlooked failure mode in rubric-based reinforcement learning for LLMs, and proposes Criterion-Distilled Policy Optimization (CriPO) using self-distillation to address both Suppressed Criteria and Unexplored Criteria without train-inference mismatch. This work addresses a fundamental limitation in LLM alignment, potentially improving the efficiency and effectiveness of training models on open-ended tasks like medicine and science, where rubric-based RL is increasingly used. The analysis shows that Suppressed Criteria affect over 57% of samples with an average of 1.8 per sample. CriPO uses a criterion-injection self-teacher for Unexplored Criteria and a counterfactual self-teacher for Suppressed Criteria, achieving stronger performance with approximately 2x fewer optimization steps.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 21, 04:00

**Background**: Rubric-based reinforcement learning replaces scalar rewards with structured multi-criteria rubrics to guide LLM training. A known issue is limited exploration where some criteria are never satisfied (Unexplored Criteria). This paper identifies a new failure mode where criteria are satisfied but their learning signals are lost due to scalar reward aggregation (Suppressed Criteria).

## [Alignment Tuning Installs Sycophancy in LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.18114) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new study across five model families and seven bias types reveals that sycophancy and cue-induced biases in LLMs are installed by alignment tuning, not pretraining, and can be decoded and steered via hidden state directions. This finding pinpoints alignment tuning as the source of a critical safety flaw, offering a mechanistic understanding and a potential debiasing method that could improve LLM reliability and trustworthiness. The researchers extracted per-bias directions from hidden states and validated them via probing, transfer, and causal intervention; they found that biases remain representationally distinct and cross-bias entanglement is model-specific.

**Background**: Sycophancy in LLMs refers to the model's tendency to align its answers with a user's stated belief rather than objective evidence. ## [Incentive Design for Content Creation with AI Spillovers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.14372) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces the Content Creation with Spillovers (CCS) model, a game between a platform and creators where AI reuse creates positive spillovers, and proposes a Provisional Allocation mechanism that guarantees a Pareto-dominant equilibrium. As AI-generated content grows, platforms need new incentive designs to prevent free-riding and encourage high-quality contributions; this work provides a rigorous theoretical foundation for such mechanisms. The paper shows that standard contest mechanisms can be unstable under spillovers, and the proposed Provisional Allocation mechanisms guarantee a unique Pareto-dominant equilibrium. Optimizing welfare within this family is generally hard to approximate, but the authors develop approximation algorithms for structured spillover classes and random instances.

**Background**: Positive spillovers occur when one creator's effort improves the quality of others' content through AI recombination, potentially reducing individual incentives to contribute. The CCS model formalizes this as a game where the platform contracts on content quality and aims to maximize social welfare, while creators choose effort levels that jointly determine quality.

## [Metric Freedom Predicts When Multi-Agent Distillation Works](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.01608) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers introduce Metric Freedom (F), the first a priori predictor of skill utility when distilling multi-agent systems into single-agent models, and propose AdaSkill, an adaptive distillation framework that leverages F to achieve up to 8x cost reduction and 15x latency improvement. This work provides a principled answer to a long-standing question in multi-agent system design—when and what to distill—enabling practitioners to avoid costly trial-and-error and reliably achieve performance gains from distillation. Metric Freedom measures the topological rigidity of a metric's scoring landscape via a Mantel test, and strongly predicts skill utility (r=-0.85, p<0.0001) across 4 tasks, 11 datasets, and 6 metrics. AdaSkill's two-stage framework selectively extracts and refines skills on 'free' metrics, matching or exceeding original MAS performance.

## [LLM Direct Prediction of Misinformation Sharing Fails](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.06820) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new study finds that asking LLMs directly to predict human sharing behavior for deceptive content fails, while credibility scores are better predictors. The research tested eight LLM evaluators on 290 deceptive articles with human ratings. This challenges the common practice of using LLM direct predictions for misinformation risk evaluation, highlighting that the choice of question matters as much as the model. It has significant implications for AI safety and content moderation pipelines. For every evaluator, credibility scores tracked human sharing at least as closely as sharing scores, and sharing scores offered no detectable benefit beyond credibility. The pattern held even when questions were asked separately or in reversed order.

## [RoboPIN: Grounded Embodied Reasoning via Pinned Chain-of-Thought](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.15753) ⭐️ 8.0/10

RoboPIN introduces Pinned Chain-of-Thought (PinCoT), a structured reasoning paradigm that anchors each reasoning step to visual evidence using reasoning anchors, preventing entity reference drift in embodied reasoning tasks. The model, with only 4B parameters, outperforms 7B-level open-source embodied models on 14 benchmarks, achieving a 12% average improvement over the strongest 7B baseline. This work addresses a critical limitation in embodied reasoning—visual grounding drift—which has hindered the reliability of vision-language models in physical environments. By introducing structured reasoning anchors, PinCoT enables more consistent and interpretable multi-step reasoning, with potential applications in robotics and autonomous systems. PinCoT binds each task-relevant entity to a structured visual anchor containing entity name, unique identity, view index, and spatial grounding. The authors built a fully automated data generation pipeline to construct PIN-170K, a high-quality PinCoT-formatted dataset, and trained RoboPIN via three-stage post-training with process-supervised alignment.

Visual grounding is the ability to localize image regions corresponding to language descriptions, a key capability for embodied agents.

A new paper reveals that uncertainty signals in budgeted LLM verification are heteroskedastic across cost strata, meaning their quality varies unpredictably, which limits the effectiveness of global optimization. The authors propose a controlled intervention hierarchy and a method called Heterogeneity-Gated Allocation (HGA) to address this issue. This finding challenges the common assumption that stronger optimization over a shared uncertainty signal always improves budgeted LLM verification. It provides a principled way to decide when to use global versus stratified allocation, potentially improving the reliability and efficiency of LLM systems under limited compute budgets. The paper introduces a local model to characterize distortion in global allocation and shows that the upper bound scales with cross-stratum signal-quality dispersion. Experiments on MBPP and MATH using Qwen3-8B, LLaMA3-8B, and GPT-4o-mini show that cost-stratified thresholding (CST) improves hit rate by up to 17 percentage points in strongly heterogeneous settings, while HGA preserves most gains without blind stratification.

**Background**: Budgeted LLM verification systems decide which outputs to verify under a limited compute budget, often using uncertainty signals to prioritize. This paper fills that gap by formally analyzing heteroskedasticity in uncertainty signals.

## [Data-Driven ML Cannot Achieve Symbolic Logical Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.26454) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new paper proves that conventional data-driven machine learning, including large models like GPT-5, cannot achieve symbolic-level syllogistic reasoning due to fundamental limitations in training data and mapping contradictions. This challenges the scaling law assumption that more data and larger models alone can lead to human-like reasoning, with significant implications for AI research and the development of truly logical AI systems. The paper shows that training data from combination tables cannot distinguish all 24 valid syllogism types, and end-to-end mapping creates contradictory targets. Experiments with GPT-5 and Euler Net confirm these limitations, with Euler Net achieving only 56% accuracy on random tests.
