## [COLIP-2: Unifying Olfaction, Vision, and Language](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.17559) ⭐️ 7.0/10

COLIP-2 is a multimodal embedding model that integrates molecular structure, gas-sensor readings, odor descriptors, and images into a shared representation space, enabling robots to probabilistically localize aromas to objects in a scene. This work advances embodied AI by adding olfaction as a first-class modality alongside vision and language, addressing a critical gap in robotic perception. It also highlights the need for large-scale paired image-scent datasets and proposes optimizations for real-time edge deployment. The model is built on contrastive pre-training similar to CLIP, but extends to four modalities: molecular structure, gas-sensor readings, odor descriptors, and images. The authors note that no ImageNet-scale paired image-scent dataset exists, and they release COLIP-2 to demonstrate the limits of current open-source olfactory data.

**Background**: Contrastive Language–Image Pre-training (CLIP) is a technique that trains two neural networks (one for images, one for text) using a contrastive objective, enabling cross-modal retrieval and generation. COLIP-2 extends this concept to olfaction, creating a joint embedding space for smell, sight, and language. Olfaction is rarely integrated into multimodal AI due to data scarcity and sensor challenges.

## [TypiCore: Hybrid Active Query for Time Series Continual Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.17632) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces TypiCore, a hybrid active query strategy that alternates between typicality-based and diversity-based sample selection for active class-incremental learning on multivariate time series, achieving statistically significant improvements over baselines. This work addresses the critical challenge of label scarcity in continual learning for time series, a domain where fully labeled data streams are rare. TypiCore matches or surpasses fully supervised performance while using only a fraction of labels, potentially reducing annotation costs in healthcare, manufacturing, and other real-world applications. TypiCore is evaluated on the TSCIL benchmark across four datasets, combining rehearsal-based continual learning with active query strategies. The hybrid approach alternates between selecting typical (representative) and diverse samples across active learning cycles to build memory buffers that are both representative and diverse.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 21, 04:00

**Background**: Continual learning (CL) aims to learn from a stream of data without forgetting previous knowledge, but most CL methods assume fully labeled data. Active class-incremental learning (ACIL) relaxes this by allowing the model to selectively query labels under a fixed annotation budget. Rehearsal-based CL stores a subset of past examples in a memory buffer to mitigate forgetting. TypiCore improves upon existing uncertainty-based and distribution-aware query strategies by combining typicality and diversity.

The paper proposes SNIP, a model-agnostic data-centric framework that prunes source nodes with structural deviations to mitigate negative transfer in unsupervised graph domain adaptation. This shifts the UGDA paradigm from feature-level alignment to data-level refinement, addressing a fundamental limitation of existing methods that assume all source nodes are beneficial. SNIP integrates multiple centrality measures to quantify structural discrepancy and uses rank-based normalization to filter out incompatible source nodes, constructing a refined sub-source graph for subsequent alignment.

**Background**: Unsupervised graph domain adaptation (UGDA) transfers knowledge from a labeled source graph to an unlabeled target graph under domain discrepancies. Existing methods align node features in latent space but ignore that source nodes with structural outliers may cause negative transfer. SNIP addresses this by selectively pruning such nodes.

## [HARR: Online Controller for Multi-Subsystem MAPD with Buffer-Limited Handover](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.17724) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper formalizes the Multi-Subsystem MAPD with Buffer-limited Handover Stations (MS-MAPD-BHS) problem and proposes HARR, an online controller that uses a shared dock reservation calendar and rolling-horizon buffer occupancy projections to coordinate agents across subsystems. This work addresses a practical challenge in modular logistics and robotics where payloads must be transferred between separate subsystems via shared handover stations. By ensuring collision-free and buffer-safe operations, HARR can significantly improve throughput and reduce backlog in real-world multi-agent transport systems. HARR accepts a candidate route only if its dock interval is free and the resulting buffer occupancy projection remains within capacity. In simulations, HARR achieved up to 77% higher throughput and 92% lower backlog compared to a fixed-dock ablation at moderate load, while also reducing planning time relative to a coupled station-aware Token Passing baseline.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 21, 04:00

**Background**: Multi-Agent Pickup and Delivery (MAPD) is an extension of Multi-Agent Path Finding (MAPF) where agents repeatedly serve pickup-and-delivery tasks in a shared environment. In lifelong MAPD, tasks arrive over time, requiring online coordination. The new problem MS-MAPD-BHS introduces multiple subsystems connected by handover stations with limited buffer capacity, adding constraints on buffer occupancy and dock availability.

## [Adaptive Digital Twin Framework Using Robust MPC](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.18164) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new framework integrates Fisher score-based drift detection, Low-Rank Adaptation (LoRA) for efficient model updates, and Mann-Whitney U test for statistical validation to maintain surrogate model fidelity under concept drift in digital twins. This work addresses a critical challenge in digital twins—maintaining model accuracy under evolving conditions—by providing a principled, computationally efficient approach that could enhance reliability in applications like additive manufacturing. The framework triggers fine-tuning of fewer than 1% of model parameters upon drift detection and statistically certifies predictive improvement before deployment. It was validated on a stochastic linear system and a directed energy deposition additive manufacturing process.

**Background**: Digital twins use surrogate models to mirror physical systems in real time, but these models can degrade due to concept drift—changes in operating conditions. This framework combines Fisher score-based drift detection, LoRA for parameter-efficient fine-tuning, and Mann-Whitney U test for validation.

## [Adaptive Safety Margins for Visual Navigation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.18200) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers propose a context-conditioned safety critic that learns adaptive safety margins for ranking diffusion-based trajectory proposals in cluttered visual navigation, achieving state-of-the-art success rates and path efficiency on HM3D and MP3D benchmarks. This work addresses a critical bottleneck in visual navigation—fixed safety margins—by introducing an adaptive approach that balances safety and efficiency, enabling robots to navigate cluttered indoor spaces more reliably without task-specific tuning. The safety critic decomposes ranking into three terms: safety (clearance-budget penalty and control-barrier-function residual), efficiency (smoothness and safety-gated detour-ratio penalty), and distance-constraint matching to prevent margin collapse. It is trained with privileged ESDF geometry in simulation and distilled into a perception-only selector via teacher-student procedure.

**Background**: Diffusion-based planners generate diverse trajectory candidates from egocentric RGB-D, but selecting the best one is challenging. This work combines diffusion planning with learned safety critics to adapt margins based on context.

Researchers propose a simple domain generalization framework for pixel-level image tampering detection in modern vision-language models (VLMs), using balanced minibatch sampling and a late-injection strategy. Their method outperforms prior state-of-the-art PIXAR by 26.1% and 26.8% relative improvement in average gIoU and cIoU across OOD VLMs. As VLMs like ChatGPT and Gemini enable advanced image editing, robust tampering detection is critical for AI safety and visual authenticity. SCA introduces a segment-wise compression method for chain-of-thought reasoning that protects answer tokens from compression, preventing answer drift while achieving state-of-the-art compression rates. This addresses a critical failure mode in existing CoT compression methods—answer drift—where compressing the answer segment degrades accuracy. SCA enables more efficient reasoning without sacrificing answer quality, benefiting applications like large language model inference. SCA parses completions into think and answer segments, routes compression rewards only to successful think tokens, and aligns answer length and distribution with a frozen base model. Experiments across multiple domains show SCA preserves base model performance while achieving SOTA compression.

## [Context-Dependent Argumentation Frameworks Introduced](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.31581) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces Context-Dependent Argumentation Frameworks (CDAFs), an extension of Dung's argumentation frameworks where a defeat function determines which attacks succeed per context, and blocked attacks are inverted rather than deleted. CDAFs enable strategic manipulation of argument acceptance through selective activation of attacks, which has implications for AI systems that need to adapt reasoning to changing contexts or external influences. The paper defines the ACTIVATION-MANIPULATION decision problem and shows it is NP-complete for grounded semantics with mandatory perspectives, with hardness arising from the activation choice itself.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 21, 04:00

**Background**: Dung's argumentation frameworks (AFs) are a foundational formalism in AI for reasoning with conflicting arguments, where arguments attack each other and extensions (sets of acceptable arguments) are computed under various semantics. Context-dependent argumentation frameworks (CDAFs) extend this by allowing the success of attacks to depend on an external context, modeled via a defeat function. A perspective-labeled specialization derives the defeat function from a relevance set and a priority, where the relevance set represents the agent's action space.

## [PhyB Unifies Uncertainty Quantification in Offline RL](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.00680) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper proposes Posterior Hybrid Bayesian Belief (PhyB), a method that unifies sample-level and model-level epistemic uncertainty quantification in offline reinforcement learning, and develops an iterative regularized policy optimization algorithm with monotonic improvement guarantees. This work addresses a fundamental challenge in offline RL—managing epistemic uncertainty—without restrictive assumptions, offering both theoretical guarantees and state-of-the-art empirical performance, which could advance practical deployment of offline RL in data-scarce scenarios. PhyB reformulates the expectation over dynamics models as a convex combination over a subset of models, and the authors prove that the objective discrepancy induced by this approximation remains bounded. The resulting algorithm provides metric-agnostic guarantees for monotonic improvement until convergence.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 21, 04:00

**Background**: Offline reinforcement learning learns policies from static datasets without environment interaction. A key challenge is epistemic uncertainty due to limited data coverage and ambiguous dynamics. Bayesian RL treats the dynamics model as a random variable to quantify uncertainty, but policy optimization is computationally hard. Prior methods either scale poorly or impose restrictive posterior assumptions.

## [Visual Access Boundaries in VLM Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.12815) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces Visual Access Sweep, a causal intervention method that masks attention from generated tokens to image tokens, and defines Visual Access Boundaries (VABs) as the minimal visual information needed for VLM reasoning. Experiments on Qwen2.5-VL and InternVL3 show that both direct answering and Chain-of-Thought (CoT) prompting have finite VABs, with CoT's VAB layer differing from no-CoT by at most two layers. This work provides empirical insights into how Vision-Language Models (VLMs) use visual information during reasoning, revealing that CoT does not primarily improve performance by prolonging direct image-token access but by extending language-side computation over image-derived hidden states. Understanding these boundaries can guide the design of more efficient and interpretable VLMs. The study tested six model configurations from Qwen2.5-VL and InternVL3, including 14B, 32B, and 38B scales. CoT gains are constrained by perceptual readout: CoT helps when the visual attribute can be reliably read out, but not when readout is unreliable; a symbolic-attribute oracle showed CoT improves counting once ground-truth attributes are supplied as text.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 21, 04:00

The Visual Access Sweep method causally intervenes by masking attention from generated tokens to image tokens across layers and time steps to identify the minimal visual access needed.

Researchers introduced SportD, a benchmark of 478 on-ball decisions from the 2022 FIFA World Cup, to evaluate whether vision-language models can make strategically optimal choices in soccer by comparing their decisions to a possession-value model. ## [SONAR: Frequency-Guided Framework for Deepfake Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.21325) ⭐️ 7.0/10

SONAR proposes a frequency-guided framework that disentangles audio into low- and high-frequency components using a contrastive learning approach, achieving state-of-the-art performance on ASVspoof 2021 and in-the-wild benchmarks while converging four times faster than strong baselines. This work addresses the critical problem of generalization in deepfake audio detection by explicitly handling spectral bias, which has been a major obstacle for existing detectors. By elevating high-frequency residuals as first-class learning signals, SONAR can be integrated into any model or modality where subtle high-frequency cues are decisive. SONAR uses an XLSR encoder for low-frequency content and a cloned path with learnable SRM value-constrained high-pass filters to distill high-frequency residuals, then reunites them via frequency cross-attention. A frequency-aware Jensen-Shannon contrastive loss pulls real content-noise pairs together while pushing fake embeddings apart.

**Background**: Deepfake audio detectors often fail to generalize to out-of-distribution inputs due to spectral bias, the tendency of neural networks to learn low-frequency patterns first and neglect high-frequency details. SONAR explicitly addresses this by disentangling frequency components and using contrastive learning to sharpen decision boundaries.

## [mHC-GNN: Manifold-Constrained Hyper-Connections for GNNs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.02451) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces mHC-GNN, a method that adapts manifold-constrained hyper-connections from Transformers to graph neural networks, expanding node representations across parallel streams and constraining stream-mixing matrices to the Birkhoff polytope of doubly stochastic matrices via Sinkhorn-Knopp normalization. mHC-GNN mitigates over-smoothing in deep GNNs and enhances expressiveness beyond the 1-Weisfeiler-Leman test, with theoretical guarantees on convergence rates, enabling GNNs to maintain high accuracy even at 128 layers. The method provides a two-regime analysis: for layers less than the number of streams, the protected orthogonal subspace prevents collapse; for deeper layers, a layer-wise contraction bound shows node-pair differences decay at rate (1-ε)^L, much slower than the standard (1-γ)^L. Experiments show mHC-GNN maintains over 74% accuracy at 128 layers, while standard GNNs collapse beyond 16 layers.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 21, 04:00

**Background**: Graph Neural Networks (GNNs) are deep learning models for graph-structured data, but they suffer from over-smoothing, where node representations become indistinguishable as layers increase, and limited expressiveness bounded by the 1-Weisfeiler-Leman (1-WL) graph isomorphism test. The Birkhoff polytope is the set of all doubly stochastic matrices, which are square matrices with nonnegative entries where each row and column sums to one; its vertices are permutation matrices.

## [Ternary Grid Fixes Gradient Suppression in QML Circuits](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.23409) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers identified a gradient suppression issue in trainable-frequency quantum circuits and proposed ternary grid initialization, setting prefactors to powers of 3, to overcome it. The method achieves median R² = 0.997 on synthetic benchmarks, far outperforming unary initialization's 0.18. This work removes a key practical barrier in trainable-frequency quantum circuits, enabling reliable function approximation for quantum machine learning. It could accelerate progress in variational quantum algorithms by ensuring gradient-driven training converges even for high-frequency targets. Ternary grid initialization sets prefactors to {1, 3, 9, ..., 3^{k-1}}, ensuring every target frequency within [−ω_max, ω_max] is within 0.5 unit of the accessible spectrum at initialization. The method was validated on two real-world benchmark datasets, consistently outperforming fixed and trainable unary baselines.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 21, 04:00

**Background**: Angle-encoded variational quantum circuits can represent functions as truncated Fourier series, but approximating high-frequency functions with fixed encoding requires many gates. Trainable-frequency circuits learn data-encoding prefactors to adapt the frequency spectrum, but suffer from gradient suppression when the target frequency is far from initialization. Ternary grid initialization mitigates this by ensuring the spectral gap does not suppress gradients.

<a id="item-459"></a>
## [DIB-OD: Robust Heterogeneous Graph Adaptation via Decoupled Info Bottleneck](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.10882) ⭐️ 7.0/10

DIB-OD introduces a decoupled information bottleneck and online distillation framework that explicitly decomposes graph representations into orthogonal invariant and redundant subspaces to preserve the invariant core for robust heterogeneous graph adaptation across domains. This work addresses a critical challenge in graph neural network pretraining: generalizing across heterogeneous domains with distribution shifts. By isolating invariant knowledge and preventing negative transfer, DIB-OD could significantly improve the robustness and transferability of graph models in real-world applications like drug discovery and social network analysis. The framework uses a teacher-student distillation mechanism based on the Information Bottleneck principle and the Hilbert-Schmidt Independence Criterion (HSIC) to isolate the invariant core. A self-adaptive semantic regularizer dynamically gates label influence based on predictive confidence to protect the invariant core during target-domain adaptation.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 21, 04:00

**Background**: Graph Neural Networks (GNNs) pretrained on large unlabeled graphs often struggle to generalize to heterogeneous domains due to distribution shifts. Existing methods typically focus on intra-domain patterns and fail to disentangle task-relevant invariant knowledge from domain-specific noise, leading to negative transfer and catastrophic forgetting. The Information Bottleneck method compresses input data while preserving relevant information, and HSIC measures statistical dependence between variables in reproducing kernel Hilbert spaces. Online distillation trains multiple models simultaneously without a fixed teacher, allowing evolving teaching signals.

## [Quantum ML vs Classical: A Comprehensive Benchmark on MNIST](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.27923) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new study systematically compares classical and quantum machine learning models (CSVM, QSVM, CCNN, QCNN) on the MNIST dataset across accuracy, runtime, parameter count, and memory usage, finding that quantum models often achieve higher accuracy or better efficiency at the cost of increased runtime. This work provides empirical evidence to inform the ongoing debate about the practical advantages of quantum machine learning, helping researchers and practitioners decide when quantum models might be beneficial despite their higher computational cost. For SVM models, QSVM achieved ~0.90 accuracy vs CSVM's ~0.85 at 1,000 samples, with higher runtime. For neural networks, CCNN and QCNN both exceeded 0.96 accuracy, but QCNN used fewer parameters and less memory at higher feature counts, though with longer runtime.

The MNIST dataset of handwritten digits is a standard benchmark for image recognition. This study compares classical and quantum versions of SVMs and CNNs on MNIST.

## [Guided LLM Use Boosts Independent Learning in Statistics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.01375) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A quasi-experimental study compared no LLM access, unrestricted LLM access, and guided LLM access in an undergraduate statistics course, finding that guided scaffolding promotes independent learning while unrestricted access may hinder it. This study provides empirical evidence that simply giving students LLM access is insufficient; structured guidance is crucial to ensure LLMs support reasoning rather than answer-getting, informing the design of AI-assisted learning environments. The four-week summer program involved three balanced conditions: no LLM, unrestricted LLM, and guided LLM (with training on reasoning-focused help-seeking, stepwise hints, verification, and ethical use). All quizzes and the delayed final exam were completed without LLM assistance to measure independent learning.

Quasi-experimental designs lack random assignment but are often used in educational research for practical reasons.

## [FAIR-Calib: PTQ for Diffusion LLMs Protects Fragile Frontier States](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06547) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers propose FAIR-Calib, a two-stage post-training quantization (PTQ) framework for diffusion large language models (dLLMs) that reweights calibration to protect fragile frontier states, reducing decision flips and post-commit mismatches. This work addresses a novel problem—stability lag in dLLMs—where early irreversible decisions are vulnerable to quantization errors, and provides a principled PTQ method that outperforms baselines on LLaDA and Dream models, enabling efficient deployment of dLLMs. FAIR-Calib uses a two-stage approach: Stage I probes a full-precision teacher to estimate a position prior combining frontier hits and masked-stage reliability; Stage II performs off-policy layer-wise calibration minimizing a reweighted hidden-state MSE, theoretically justified as a surrogate for output KL divergence.

**Background**: Post-training quantization (PTQ) reduces model size and inference cost by mapping FP32 activations to lower precision (e.g., INT8). Diffusion large language models (dLLMs) iteratively refine tokens but commit them irreversibly, creating a 'write frontier' where early decisions remain fragile. Quantization errors at this frontier can flip borderline decisions, which then become permanently locked and amplified.

## [TRIAGE: Role-Typed Credit Assignment for Agentic RL](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.32017) ⭐️ 7.0/10

TRIAGE introduces a role-typed credit assignment framework that classifies action segments into semantic roles (decisive progress, useful exploration, no-progress infrastructure, regression) and assigns bounded process rewards, improving credit allocation over standard GRPO. This addresses fundamental limitations of outcome-only credit in agentic RL, where useful exploration is punished in failed rollouts and redundant actions are reinforced in successful ones, potentially improving training efficiency and success rates across diverse agentic tasks. TRIAGE uses a structured judge to classify segments and applies fixed role-conditioned rules to map labels to bounded process rewards, approximating the Bayes-optimal L2 projection of per-segment advantage residuals onto the role variable. Experiments on ALFWorld, Search-QA, and WebShop show success rate improvements over GRPO and baselines, with regression detection being the dominant contributor.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 21, 04:00

**Background**: Agentic reinforcement learning trains LLMs to perform environment-facing actions like searches and clicks. Standard GRPO uses the final verifier outcome as a uniform advantage over all action tokens, which can punish useful exploration in failed rollouts and reinforce redundant actions in successful ones. TRIAGE introduces semantic role typing to provide more granular credit assignment.

## [Jetson-PI: Efficient VLA Deployment on Low-Power Devices](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.12659) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Jetson-PI introduces a foresight-aligned asynchronous correction method to deploy Vision-Language-Action (VLA) models on NVIDIA Jetson Orin, achieving 8.66x and 5.41x improvements in control frequency over naive PyTorch and vla.cpp, respectively. This work addresses the critical challenge of deploying computationally heavy VLA models on low-power edge devices, enabling real-time robot control with higher frequency and accuracy, which is essential for practical robotics applications. The method uses a lightweight future correction module to predict future environment states, mitigating perception-execution misalignment, and employs confidence-based scheduling optimization along with system-level accelerations like CUDA graph reuse and GPU-resident intermediate buffering.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 21, 04:00

**Background**: Vision-Language-Action (VLA) models combine visual and language understanding to generate robot actions, but their high computational cost limits deployment on low-power onboard devices. Asynchronous inference can hide latency by overlapping computation and execution, but introduces misalignment and reaction time issues. Jetson-PI targets these problems for the NVIDIA Jetson Orin platform.

Boogu-Image-0.1, an open-source multimodal model family, was released with Base, Turbo, Edit, and Edit-Turbo variants, achieving competitive text-to-image generation, fast inference, instruction-based editing, and bilingual text rendering under a minimal compute budget of approximately $400K training cost. This work demonstrates that strengthening understanding capabilities via a stronger encoder, agentic prompt rewriting, and inference-time scaling can substantially boost generation performance even with limited compute, advancing open-source multimodal AI and challenging closed-source systems. The model was trained on only 208.62 million unique images, and its theoretical training cost is roughly $400K. The authors release weights, code, and recipes under Apache 2.0, and share practical discussions for the research community.

Boogu-Image leverages this technique along with a stronger multimodal encoder and agentic prompt rewriting to enhance generation from understanding.

## [Tabular Foundation Models for Discrete Choice Estimation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.13314) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper identifies structural limitations of tabular foundation models (TFMs) for discrete choice estimation and proposes a reformulation that encodes choice-set dependence and individual heterogeneity, achieving better accuracy and a 16x speedup over hierarchical Bayesian methods. This work bridges the gap between foundation models and discrete choice modeling, a core demand estimation framework in marketing and operations, enabling faster and more accurate large-scale demand estimation without task-specific training. The best reformulation outperforms hierarchical Bayesian estimation on holdout log-likelihood and hit rate, with the largest advantage in the medium-data regime (10–40 purchase occasions per consumer). Fine-tuning on population choice data further improves performance for consumers with shallow purchase histories.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 21, 04:00

**Background**: Tabular foundation models (TFMs) are pretrained transformer networks that perform in-context learning on structured data without task-specific fine-tuning. Discrete choice estimation models how individuals choose among a set of alternatives, a key problem in demand estimation. Hierarchical Bayesian methods are a standard approach but are computationally expensive. This paper addresses the structural mismatch between TFMs' row-independent assumption and the set-valued, heterogeneous nature of discrete choice data.

## [VideoSEMA: Efficient Mamba-like Attention for Video Understanding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.14711) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers propose VideoSEMA, a video understanding model that combines a Mamba-like attention block (SEMA) with softmax temporal attention, achieving state-of-the-art results on K400 and SSv2 benchmarks. VideoSEMA demonstrates that Mamba-like architectures can outperform heavier vision transformers and Mamba models in video understanding, while scaling gracefully to high resolutions, which is crucial for real-world video analysis. The SEMA block applies local window attention in parallel with global averaging, and under certain rank conditions, split space-time attention is proven equivalent to full space-time attention. Without fine-tuning, VideoSEMA degrades much more gracefully than VideoMamba when resolution scales from 224^2 to 1024^2.

**Background**: Video understanding often relies on transformers with full space-time attention, which is computationally expensive. Mamba is a deep learning architecture that uses selective state space models for efficient sequence modeling, offering a competitive alternative to transformers. VideoSEMA adapts Mamba-like principles to video tasks by combining local and global attention in a split space-time design.

## [Muon Optimizer Boosts Agentic RL by 88% on ALFWorld](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.16169) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new study shows that the Muon optimizer, when applied only to hidden weight matrices, improves final-window validation success rate from 0.290 to 0.546 (+88%) compared to AdamW in sparse-reward agentic RL on ALFWorld using Qwen2.5-0.5B-Instruct and GiGPO. This finding challenges the dominance of AdamW in RL post-training and suggests that optimizer choice can significantly impact agentic RL performance, potentially leading to faster and more sample-efficient training of language-model-based agents. The effect is sensitive to the advantage estimator and learning rate; at 3e-5, Muon improves GRPO from 0.161 to 0.268, while at 1e-5, GraphGPO Muon reaches 0.901 success and achieves 0.5 and 0.75 success 30 and 60 updates earlier, respectively.

RouteCost addresses limitations of static lookup methods and monolithic regressors by incorporating destination demand mix, dimensional pricing, and operational effects like shipment consolidation, potentially reducing cost surprises and improving profitability. The framework consists of four stages: time-aware demand forecasting, fee-card-informed baseline pricing, residual correction using a second-stage model, and proxy-based box-consolidation inference. Route-level estimates are aggregated via a route-weighted expectation formulation to produce product-level predictions.

**Background**: In e-commerce, shipping costs depend not only on distance but also on destination demand mix, billable weight (actual vs. dimensional weight), surcharges, and operational factors like shipment consolidation. Traditional static lookup tables miss these variations, while monolithic machine learning models may exploit non-causal correlations. RouteCost decomposes the problem into interpretable stages to improve accuracy and explainability.

This paper establishes a square-root threshold for the number of selected positions in a Gaussian random-field model, below which total correlation vanishes and above which it persists with positive probability. This provides a rigorous theoretical foundation for understanding dependence costs in parallel decoding of masked diffusion models, potentially improving efficiency in generative AI. The model uses a locally dependent nonnegative score field and distance-dependent Gaussian correlation; the threshold occurs at the square-root scale of the number of positions.

This paper introduces a polynomial-time algorithm for multi-round resource allocation with stochastic arrivals, using a population-level surrogate value function to overcome intractable Bellman recursion. This work provides a computationally feasible solution for sequential resource allocation problems with stochastic referrals, which has applications in adaptive network recruitment and similar domains where diminishing returns and endogenous future opportunities are present. The algorithm achieves polynomial complexity in the total budget by employing truncated probability generating functions within an exact dynamic program.
